Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})=\typecolon\prod_{r=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{\bar{k}}\left(\frac{1}{(m_{r}-1)!}\frac{d^{m_{r}-1}\alpha_{r}(x)}{dx^{m_{r}-1}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{m}_{s}-1)!}\frac{d^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}\beta_{s}(\bar{x})}{d\bar{x}^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}}\right)Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\typecolon\,,

(170)

where the normal ordering
\typecolon\typecolon
is defined as

\begin{split}&\typecolon\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\,\alpha^{\lambda^{\prime}}(q)\typecolon=\typecolon\alpha^{\lambda^{\prime}}(q)\,\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\typecolon=\begin{cases}\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\,\alpha^{\lambda^{\prime}}(q)&p\leq q\,,\\
\alpha^{\lambda^{\prime}}(q)\,\alpha^{\lambda}(p)&p\geq q\,,\end{cases}\\
&\typecolon\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\mathrm{e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\typecolon=\typecolon\mathrm{e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\,\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\typecolon=\mathrm{e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\,\alpha^{\lambda}(p)\,,\\
&\typecolon x^{\alpha^{\lambda}}\,{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\typecolon=\typecolon{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\,x^{\alpha^{\lambda}}\typecolon={\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\,x^{\alpha^{\lambda}}\,,\end{split}

(171)

and similarly for
\beta^{\lambda}
and
\bar{x}^{\beta^{\lambda}}
. The vertex operator for general vectors in
V_{\Lambda}
is defined by linear extension to all of
V_{\Lambda}
.

{remark}

Using the central extension (
156
), (
170
) can be used to define vertex operators even if
{\rm e}^{\lambda}\in\mathbb{C}[\Lambda]
. These vertex operators will act on vectors of the form (
152
) with
{\rm e}^{\lambda}\in\mathbb{C}[\Lambda]
rather than
\mathbb{C}[\Lambda_{0}]
. This will be crucial when we construct module vertex operators and intertwining operators on the modules of
V_{\Lambda}
.

The vacuum vector is given by
\mathbf{1}=\mathrm{e}^{0}
. The conformal vector is constructed below, see (
187
).

3.3
Proof of axioms

We now prove that
(V_{\Lambda},Y_{V_{\Lambda}},\omega_{L},\omega_{R},\mathbf{1})
is a non-chiral VOA.

Proof of identity property
1
:
From the definition (
165
), it is clear that
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(\mathbf{1},x,\bar{x})={\rm e}_{0}=\mathds{1}.

Proof of grading-restriction property
2
:
The grading on
V_{\Lambda}
is given by defining the conformal weight of vector
v
of the form (
152
) by

h_{v}=\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i},\quad\bar{h}_{v}=\frac{\langle\beta,\beta\rangle}{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{k}}\bar{m}_{j}\,,

(172)

where
m_{i}
and
\bar{m}_{j}
are positive integers appearing in (
152
).
Note that for
{\rm e}^{\lambda}
with
\lambda=(\alpha,\beta)\in\Lambda_{0}
, we have
(\alpha,0)\in\Lambda
,
(\alpha,0)\circ(\alpha,0)=\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Then we have that
h_{v},\bar{h}_{v}\geq 0
so that
V_{(h,\bar{h})}=0
for
h
or
\bar{h}<0
, i.e.
M=0
in (
43
).

Similarly,
\langle\beta,\beta\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, which implies that both
h_{v}
and
\bar{h}_{v}
are
positive integers
16
16

16

Note that this argument also works for a general
{\rm e}^{\lambda}\in\mathbb{C}[\Lambda]
with
\lambda\in\Lambda
since
\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle,\langle\beta,\beta\rangle\geq 0
even in this case.
.

We will now show that dim
(V_{(h,\bar{h})})<\infty
.
Note that
\Lambda_{1}^{0}
and
\Lambda_{2}^{0}
are lattices. It suffices to show that there exist only finitely many vectors of the form (
152
), satisfying the conditions in (
172
). We first show that for any
h,\bar{h}\in\mathbb{R}
the number of distinct
\lambda=(\alpha,\beta)\in\Lambda_{0}
satisfying

\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle\leq 2h\text{ and }\langle\beta,\beta\rangle\leq 2\bar{h}\,,\text{ where }\alpha\in\Lambda_{1}^{0},\beta\in\Lambda_{2}^{0}\,,

(173)

can be only finitely many. Consider the sets

X_{1}=\{\alpha\in\Lambda_{1}^{0}\penalty\ \lvert\penalty\ \langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle\leq 2h\},

(174)

X_{2}=\{\beta\in\Lambda_{2}^{0}\penalty\ \lvert\penalty\ \langle\beta,\beta\rangle\leq 2\bar{h}\},

(175)

which have finite cardinality, say
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
, due to the fact that
\Lambda_{1}^{0}
and
\Lambda_{2}^{0}
are discrete. Then the set

X=\{\lambda=(\alpha,\beta)\in\Lambda_{0}\penalty\ \lvert\penalty\ \langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle\leq 2h,\penalty\ \langle\beta,\beta\rangle\leq 2\bar{h}\}

(176)

is finite because the map

\begin{split}&X\longrightarrow X_{1}\times X_{2}\\
&\lambda=(\alpha,\beta)\longmapsto(\alpha,\beta)\end{split}

(177)

is injective. More precisely
\#X\leq N_{1}N_{2}
.
Now, as there are only finitely many combinations of positive integers
\{m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
and
\{\bar{m}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\bar{k}}
such that

h_{v}-\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i},\quad\bar{h}_{v}-\frac{\langle\beta,\beta\rangle}{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{k}}\bar{m}_{j}\,,

(178)

hence there are only finitely many generating vectors possible, which implies that dim
(V_{h,\bar{h}})<\infty
.

Proof of single-valuedness property
3
:
For the general vector
v
of the form (
152
) we have

\begin{split}h_{v}-\bar{h}_{v}&=\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle-\langle\beta,\beta\rangle}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{k}}\bar{m}_{j}\\
&=\frac{\lambda\circ\lambda}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{k}}\bar{m}_{j}\in\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

(179)

where we used the fact that
\Lambda
is an even Lorentzian lattice.

Proof of creation property
4
:

We want to show that for any state
v\in V_{\Lambda}
,

\lim_{x,\,\bar{x}\to 0}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})\,\mathbf{1}=v.

(180)

Let us first consider the case when
v={\rm e}^{\lambda}
, then the
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}
operator is given in (
165
). One then has to expand the exponentials, we ignore the terms when
\alpha^{\lambda}(n)
and
\beta^{\lambda}(n)
have
n>0
, as they annihilate
\mathbb{C}[\Lambda_{0}]
. The two exponentials that remain will only have positive powers of
x
and
\bar{x}
, which vanish when we take the limit. Hence

\lim_{x,\,\bar{x}\to 0}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\,\mathbf{1}={\rm e}_{\lambda}\cdot\mathbf{1}.

(181)

Here, we used the fact that
\mathbf{1}={\rm e}^{0}
so that the action of
x^{\alpha^{\lambda}}(\bar{x}^{\beta^{\lambda}})
on this is by identity, since
\langle\alpha^{\lambda},0\rangle=0\left(\langle\beta^{\lambda},0\rangle=0\right)
. Hence

\lim_{x,\,\bar{x}\to 0}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\,\mathbf{1}={\rm e}_{\lambda}\,{\rm e}^{0}=(-1)^{\epsilon(0,\lambda)}{\rm e}^{\lambda}={\rm e}^{\lambda}\,,

(182)

where we have used (
154
), (
160
) and that
{\rm e}_{\lambda}=(1,\lambda)
.
We now prove (
180
) for a general vector
v
of the form (
152
). The normal ordering in the definition (
170
) and the fact that
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{+}
annihilates
\mathbb{C}[\Lambda_{0}]
forces the product to take the form

\frac{d^{m_{r}-1}\alpha_{r}(x)}{dx^{m_{r}-1}}\to\sum_{p_{r}\leq-m_{r}}(m_{r}-1)!\alpha_{r}(p_{r})x^{-p_{r}-m_{r}},

(183)

and

\frac{d^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}\beta_{s}(\bar{x})}{d\bar{x}^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}}\to\sum_{q_{s}\leq-\bar{m}_{s}}(\bar{m}_{s}-1)!\beta_{s}(q_{s})\bar{x}^{-q_{s}-\bar{m}_{s}}.

(184)

Thus we have

\begin{split}&\typecolon\prod_{r=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{\bar{k}}\left(\frac{1}{(m_{r}-1)!}\frac{d^{m_{r}-1}\alpha_{r}(x)}{dx^{m_{r}-1}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{m}_{s}-1)!}\frac{d^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}\beta_{s}(\bar{x})}{d\bar{x}^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}}\right)Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\typecolon\mathbf{1}\\
&\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\leq-m_{1}\\
...\\
p_{k}\leq-m_{k}\end{subarray}}\typecolon\alpha_{1}(p_{1})\alpha_{2}(p_{2})\dots\alpha_{k}(p_{k})x^{-(p_{1}+\dots p_{k})-(m_{1}+\dots+m_{k})}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}\leq-\bar{m}_{1}\\
...\\
q_{\bar{k}}\leq-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}}\end{subarray}}\beta_{1}(q_{1})\beta_{2}(q_{2})\dots\beta_{\bar{k}}(q_{\bar{k}})\bar{x}^{-(q_{1}+\dots q_{\bar{k}})-(\bar{m}_{1}+\dots+\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\typecolon\mathbf{1}.\end{split}

(185)

When we take
x,\bar{x}\to 0
only the
p_{r}=-m_{r},q_{s}=-\bar{m}_{s}
terms in the sum survives. Combining this fact with the proof of (
180
) for
v={\rm e}^{\lambda}
, we get

\begin{split}\lim_{x,\bar{x}\to 0}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})\mathbf{1}\\
&=\left(\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdot\alpha_{2}(-m_{2})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdot\beta_{2}(-\bar{m}_{2})\cdots\beta_{\ell}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda}=v,\end{split}

(186)

where we also use the fact that
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})
can only contribute terms with
x^{n}
and
\bar{x}^{m}
, where
n
and
m
are greater than 0.

Proof of Virasoro property
5
:
The conformal vector is given by

\omega_{\Lambda}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{1})}\left(u_{i}(-1)^{2}\right)\otimes\mathbf{1}\,+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{2})}\left(v_{i}(-1)^{2}\right)\otimes\mathbf{1}\equiv\omega_{L}+\omega_{R},

(187)

where
u_{i}\in\Lambda_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C},v_{i}\in\Lambda_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}
are orthonormal basis of
\mathfrak{h}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{2}
respectively
17
17

17

Note that a different choice of orthonormal basis will give isomorphic LLVOAs. Indeed if
\{u_{i}^{\prime}\}
and
\{v_{j}^{\prime}\}
are orthonormal bases of
\mathfrak{h}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{2}
respectively, different from
\{u_{i}\}
of and
\{v_{j}\}
. Then the map
f:V_{\Lambda}\longrightarrow V_{\Lambda}
which acts trivially on
\mathbb{C}[\Lambda_{0}]
and maps
u_{i}\mapsto u_{i}^{\prime},\penalty\ v_{i}\mapsto v_{i}^{\prime}
is a non-chiral VOA isomorphism.
:

\langle u_{i},u_{j}\rangle=\delta_{i,j},\quad\langle v_{i},v_{j}\rangle=\delta_{i,j}.

(188)

Since an integral basis of
\Lambda
is also a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{m,n}
, it is clear that
\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{1})=m
and
\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{2})=n
.
One can check that
the conformal vertex operator is given by

\begin{split}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(\omega,x,\bar{x})&=Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(\omega_{L},x,\bar{x})+Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(\omega_{R},x,\bar{x})\\
&=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}L_{\Lambda}(p)x^{-p-2}+\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(p)\bar{x}^{-p-2},\end{split}

(189)

where the Virasoro generators are given by
(
Frenkel:1988xz
, Section 8.7)

\begin{split}L_{\Lambda}(p)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\typecolon u_{i}(k)u_{i}(p-k)\typecolon\\
\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(p)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\typecolon v_{i}(k)v_{i}(p-k)\typecolon.\end{split}

(190)

Using the Lie brackets

\begin{split}\left[\,u_{i}(p),u_{j}(q)\,\right]&=p\,\delta_{i,j}\,\delta_{p+q,0}\,\textbf{k},\\
\left[\,v_{i}(p),v_{j}(q)\,\right]&=p\,\delta_{i,j}\,\delta_{p+q,0}\,\bar{\textbf{k}},\end{split}

(191)

one can show that the Virasoro generators indeed satisfy the Virasoro algebra (
47
) with central charge
m=\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{1}),n=\text{dim}(\mathfrak{h}_{2})
respectively, see
(
Frenkel:1988xz
, Chapter 2)
.

Proof of grading property
6
:
From (
190
) and normal ordering (
171
), we have

\begin{split}&L_{\Lambda}(0)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\typecolon u_{i}(r)u_{i}(-r)\typecolon=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{r>0}u_{i}(-r)u_{i}(r)+\frac{1}{2}u_{i}(0)^{2},\\
&\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\typecolon v_{i}(r)v_{i}(-r)\typecolon=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{r>0}v_{i}(-r)v_{i}(r)+\frac{1}{2}v_{i}(0)^{2}.\end{split}

(192)

Then for
v\in V_{\Lambda}
of the form (
152
), using the Lie bracket (
191
) and the action (
153
), we have

\begin{split}L_{\Lambda}(0)v&=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[\cdots\left(m_{j}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle u_{i},\alpha_{j}\rangle u_{i}(-m_{j})\right)\cdots\right]\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle u_{i},\alpha\rangle^{2}v\,,\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{k}m_{j}\left[\cdots\alpha_{j}(-m_{j})\cdots\right]\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle\langle u_{i},\alpha\rangle u_{i},\alpha\rangle v\,,\\
&=\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}m_{j}+\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}\right]v\,,\end{split}

(193)

where we used the fact that
\{u_{i}\}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathfrak{h}_{1}
. Similarly

\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0)v=\left[\sum_{\bar{j}=1}^{\bar{k}}\bar{m}_{\bar{j}}+\frac{\langle\beta,\beta\rangle}{2}\right]v\,.

(194)

Proof of
L(0)
-property
7
:
Let
(\alpha,\beta)\in\Lambda_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\alpha(x)^{\pm}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\pm}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left[\typecolon u_{i}(s)u_{i}(-s)\typecolon,\alpha(r)\right]x^{-r-1}

(195)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\pm}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{s\geq 1}\left[u_{i}(-s)u_{i}(s),\alpha(r)\right]+\left[u_{i}(0)^{2},\alpha(r)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 142.26378pt+\sum_{s\leq-1}\left[u_{i}(s)u_{i}(-s),\alpha(r)\right]\right)x^{-r-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\pm}}\sum_{s\neq 0}\left(s\alpha(-s)\delta_{s+r,0}-n\alpha(s)\right)\delta_{r-s,0}x^{-r-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\pm}}\sum_{s\neq 0}n\alpha(-s)\delta_{s+r,0}x^{-r-1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\pm}}r\alpha(r)x^{-r-1},

where we used

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left[u_{i}(-s)u_{i}(s),\alpha(r)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}u_{i}(-s)\left[u_{i}(s),\alpha(r)\right]+\left[u_{i}(-s),\alpha(r)\right]u_{i}(s)

(196)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(s\left\langle u_{i},\alpha\right\rangle\delta_{s+r,0}u_{i}(-s)-s\left\langle u_{i},\alpha\right\rangle\delta_{r-s,0}u_{i}(s)\right)

\displaystyle=s\alpha(-s)\delta_{s+r,0}-s\alpha(s)\delta_{r-s,0}.

Rearranging terms, we get

\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\alpha(x)^{\pm}\right]=x\frac{d\alpha(x)^{\pm}}{dx}+\alpha(x)^{\pm}.

(197)

Similarly

\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),\beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}\right]=\bar{x}\frac{d}{d\bar{x}}\beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}+\beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}.

(198)

Note that the same proof also shows that

\begin{split}&\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\alpha(x)\right]=x\frac{d}{dx}\alpha(x)+\alpha(x)\\
&\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),\beta(\bar{x})\right]=\bar{x}\frac{d}{d\bar{x}}\beta(\bar{x})+\beta(\bar{x}).\end{split}

(199)

Next using (
197
) we have

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right]}

\displaystyle=\int dx\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\alpha(x)^{\pm}\right]

(200)

\displaystyle=\int dx\left(x\frac{d}{dx}\alpha(x)^{\pm}+\alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)

\displaystyle=x\alpha(x)^{\pm},

where we used integration by parts for the formal integration.
Similarly

\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}\right]=\bar{x}\beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}.

(201)

By
\mathrm{BCH}
formula (
224
) we have

\displaystyle L_{\Lambda}(0)\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\left[\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)^{n},L_{\Lambda}(0)\right]

(202)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)L_{\Lambda}(0)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\left[\left(-\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)^{n},L_{\Lambda}(0)\right].

By (
200
) and the fact that
[\alpha(r),\alpha(s)]=0
for
r,s\leq 0
or
r,s\geq 0
, we get

\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)\right]=x\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{\pm}\right)\alpha(x)^{\pm}.

(203)

Similarly

\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(x)^{\pm}\right)\right]=\bar{x}\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}\right)\beta(\bar{x})^{\pm}.

(204)

Finally, it is clear that for
\lambda^{\prime}=\left(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\right)
and
u\in S\left(\mathfrak{h}^{-}\right)
we have

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),{\rm e}_{\lambda}x^{\alpha}\right]\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\epsilon\left(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\right)}\left(\frac{\left\langle\alpha+\alpha^{\prime},\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}\right\rangle}{2}-\frac{\left\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\right\rangle}{2}\right)x^{\left\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\right\rangle}\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}}\right)

(205)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}+\left\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\right\rangle\right)(-1)^{\epsilon(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})}x^{\left\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\right\rangle}\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}{\rm e}_{\lambda}x^{\alpha}+{\rm e}_{\lambda}x\frac{d}{dx}x^{\alpha}\right)\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)

Putting all this together, we obtain

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right)\right]=}

\displaystyle x\frac{d}{dx}\left[\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{+}\right)\right]

(206)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{+}\right){\rm e}_{\lambda}x^{\alpha}\bar{x}^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int dx\penalty\ \alpha(x)^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int d\bar{x}\penalty\ \beta(\bar{x})^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}{\rm e}_{\lambda}x^{\alpha}+{\rm e}_{\lambda}x\frac{d}{dx}x^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=x\frac{d}{dx}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right)+\frac{\left\langle\alpha,\alpha\right\rangle}{2}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right).

Similarly

\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right)\right]=\bar{x}\frac{d}{d\bar{x}}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right)+\frac{\left\langle\beta,\beta\right\rangle}{2}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x}\right).

(207)

For general vertex operators, we observe that

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),\frac{d^{r}}{dx^{r}}\alpha(x)\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{r}}{dx^{r}}\left(x\frac{d}{dx}\alpha(x)+\alpha(x)\right)

(208)

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{r-1}}{dx^{r-1}}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\alpha(x)+x\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}\alpha(x)\right)+\frac{d^{r}}{dx^{r}}\alpha(x)

\displaystyle=x\frac{d^{r}}{dx^{r}}\alpha(x)+(r+1)\frac{d^{r}}{dx^{r}}\alpha(x).

This implies that for a general vector of the form (
152
) we have

\displaystyle{\left[L_{\Lambda}(0),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})\right]}

\displaystyle=x\frac{d}{dx}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}+\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle}{2}\right)Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})

(209)

\displaystyle=x\frac{d}{dx}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})+Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left(L_{\Lambda}(0)v,x,\bar{x}\right).

Similarly

\left[\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})\right]=\bar{x}\frac{d}{d\bar{x}}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})+Y_{V_{\Lambda}}\left(\bar{L}_{\Lambda}(0)v,x,\bar{x}\right).

(210)

Proof of translation property
8
:
Observe that

\begin{split}\left[L_{\Lambda}(-1),\alpha(-r)\right]=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}[\typecolon u_{i}(s)u_{i}(-1-s)\typecolon,\alpha(-r)]\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}[u_{i}(-1-s)\,u_{i}(s),\alpha(-r)]\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigl(u_{i}(-1-s)[u_{i}(s),\alpha(-r)]+[u_{i}(-1-s),\alpha(-r)]\,u_{i}(s)\Bigr)\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigl(s\delta_{s-r,0}\langle u_{i},\alpha\rangle\,u_{i}(-1-s)-(1+s)\delta_{r+s+1,0}\langle u_{i},\alpha\rangle\,u_{i}(s)\Bigr)\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(r\,\alpha(-1-r)-(-r)\,\alpha(-1-r)\Bigr)=r\,\alpha(-\,r-1).\end{split}

(211)

Using the above commutator, it is easy to see that

\begin{split}&L_{\Lambda}(-1)\left(\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdot\beta_{2}(-\bar{m}_{2})\cdots\beta_{\bar{k}}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes{\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdots\beta_{\bar{k}}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes L_{\Lambda}(-1){\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}\left(\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdots\alpha_{i}(-1-m_{i})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdots\beta_{\bar{k}}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes{\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}.\end{split}

(212)

Now since

\begin{split}L_{\Lambda}(-1){\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\typecolon u_{i}(s)u_{i}(-1-s)\typecolon{\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{m}u_{i}(0)u_{i}(-1){\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle u_{i},\alpha\rangle u_{i}(-1){\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&=\alpha(-1){\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)},\end{split}

(213)

hence we get the action of
L_{\Lambda}(-1)
on generating vectors
v
of the form (
152
) to be:

\begin{split}L_{\Lambda}(-1)v&=\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}\left(\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdots\alpha_{i}(-1-m_{i})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdots\beta_{\bar{k}}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes{\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&+\left(\alpha(-1)\alpha_{1}(-m_{1})\cdots\alpha_{i}(-1-m_{i})\cdots\alpha_{k}(-m_{k})\,\beta_{1}(-\bar{m}_{1})\cdots\beta_{\bar{k}}(-\bar{m}_{\bar{k}})\right)\otimes{\rm e}^{(\alpha,\beta)}.\end{split}

(214)

The proof of the translation property now follows from exact same calculation as in
(
huang
, Proposition 2.2)
.

3.3.1
Proof of locality of vertex operators

In this section, we will prove the locality of two vertex operators and defer the proof of product of multiple vertex operators to Appendix
B
.

{prop}

The vertex operators
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})
for
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
satisfy the locality property
9
. More precisely there exists, multi-valued, operator-valued functions
f(z_{1},z_{2})
and
g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})
analytic in
z_{1},z_{2}
and
\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}
respectively with possible singularities at
\{(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\,\lvert\,z_{1},z_{2}\neq 0,z_{1}\neq z_{2}\}
, such that
f(z_{1},z_{2})g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})
is single-valued when
\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}
are the complex conjugates of
z_{1},z_{2}
respectively and equals

\begin{split}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\ {\textrm{w}hen}\ \lvert z_{1}\rvert>\lvert z_{2}\rvert\penalty\ ,\\
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\ {\textrm{w}hen}\ \lvert z_{2}\rvert>\lvert z_{1}\rvert\penalty\ .\end{split}

(215)

{proof}

We will closely follow the proofs of results in
(
huang
, Section 2)
. We begin by proving that

\begin{split}\left[\alpha(x_{1}),\alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})\right]=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\left[(x_{2}-x_{1})^{-2}-(-x_{2}+x_{1})^{-2}\right]\\
\left[\beta(\bar{x}_{1}),\beta^{\prime}(\bar{x}_{2})\right]=\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\left[(\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{x}_{1})^{-2}-(-\bar{x}_{2}+\bar{x}_{1})^{-2}\right]\end{split}

(216)

where
\lambda=(\alpha,\beta),\lambda^{\prime}=(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
.
We have

\displaystyle{\left[\alpha(x_{1}),\alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s\in\mathbb{Z}}[\alpha(r),\alpha^{\prime}(s)]x_{1}^{-r-1}x_{2}^{-s-1}

(217)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\,r\,\delta_{r+s,0}\,x_{1}^{-r-1}x_{2}^{-s-1}

\displaystyle=-\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}s\,x_{1}^{s-1}x_{2}^{-s-1}

\displaystyle=-\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}x_{1}^{s}x_{2}^{-s-1}

\displaystyle=-\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\left(\left(x_{1}-x_{2}\right)^{-1}-\left(-x_{2}+x_{1}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\left(\left(x_{1}-x_{2}\right)^{-2}-\left(-x_{2}+x_{1}\right)^{-2}\right),

Note that this commutator is also true for complex variables
x_{1}=z_{1},x_{2}=z_{2}
. Indeed from (
30
)

\begin{split}\alpha(z_{1})\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})&=\typecolon\alpha(z_{1})\alpha(z_{2})\typecolon+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}sz_{1}^{-s-1}z_{2}^{s-1}\\
&=\typecolon\alpha(z_{1})\alpha(z_{2})\typecolon+\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}},\quad|z_{1}|>|z_{2}|,\\
&\end{split}

(218)

and

\begin{split}\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\alpha(z_{1})&=\typecolon\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\alpha(z_{1})\typecolon-\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha\rangle\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}sz_{2}^{-s-1}z_{1}^{s-1}\\
&=\typecolon\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\alpha(z_{1})\typecolon-\frac{\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha\rangle}{(z_{2}-z_{1})^{2}},\quad|z_{2}|>|z_{1}|.\end{split}

(219)

It is easy to see that

\typecolon\alpha(z_{1})\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\typecolon=\typecolon\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\alpha(z_{1})\typecolon\penalty\ ,

(220)

which gives us the commutator. In particular

\left[\alpha(z_{1}),\alpha^{\prime}(z_{2})\right]=0.

(221)

The other Lie bracket in (
216
) can be proved similarly.
Using (

), we can show that
18
18

18

we will use
\exp
and
{\rm e}

\left[\alpha^{\prime}\left(x_{2}\right)^{-},{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}\right]=\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{x_{1}-x_{2}}{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}\penalty\ .

(222)

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\left(\,\log(x_1 - x_2)-\log(x_1)\,\right){\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\log\left(1-\frac{x_{2}}{x_{1}}\right){\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}.

One can write analogous formulas for

\begin{split}\exp\left(-\int dx_{2}\penalty\ \alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int dx_{1}\penalty\ \alpha(x_{1})^{+}\right)&\exp\left(\int dx_{2}\penalty\ \alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})^{-}\right)\\

(226)

To show the locality of vertex operator, we will also require the identities

(228)

We now have

We will often write

\begin{split}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x_{1},\bar{x}_{1})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})\\

\begin{split}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x_{1},\bar{x}_{1})\\
&=\left(x_{2}-x_{2}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{x}_{1}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\exp\left(\int\alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int\alpha(x_{1})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int\alpha^{\prime}(x_{2})^{+}\right)\\

(233)

where we used (
159
). Note that
(-x_{1}+x_{2})^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}=(x_{2}-x_{1})^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}
when
20
20

20

Recall that when
s\in\mathbb{C}
,

is to be expanded in positive integral powers of

as in (

).

\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
. To prove locality, we take complex variables
x_{1}=z_{1},x_{2}=z_{2}
and
\bar{x}_{1}=\bar{z}_{1},\bar{x}_{2}=\bar{z}_{2}
. Note that when we plug complex variable in place of formal variable, we must consider
(x_{1}-x_{2})^{s}
as a formal series so that

\begin{split}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})=\left(\sum_{p\geq 0}(-1)^{p}z_{1}^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle-p}z_{2}^{p}\right)&\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}\bar{z}_{1}^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle-q}\bar{z}_{2}^{q}\right)\\
&\quad\times F(z_{1},z_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}),\end{split}

(234)

and similarly
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})
. To complete the proof of locality,
consider the operator valued functions

\begin{split}f(z_{1},z_{2})=\exp\left(\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\log(z_1-z_2)\right)F(z_{1},z_{2}),\\
g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})=\exp\left(\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\log(\bar{z}_1-\bar{z}_2)\right)\bar{F}(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})\,.\end{split}

(235)

Then by (
30
) for
|z_{1}|>|z_{2}|
we see that

f(z_{1},z_{2})g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})=\left(\sum_{p\geq 0}(-1)^{p}z_{1}^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle-p}z_{2}^{p}\right)\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}\bar{z}_{1}^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle-q}\bar{z}_{2}^{q}\right)F(z_{1},z_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}).

(236)

For
|z_{2}|>|z_{1}|
we have

\begin{split}f(z_{1}&,z_{2})g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})=\exp\left(\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\log(-(z_2-z_1))\right)\exp\left(\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\log(-(\bar{z}_2-\bar{z}_1))\right)\\
=&\,{\rm e}^{i\pi(\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle)}\exp\left(\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\log(z_2-z_1)\right)\exp\left(\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\log(\bar{z}_2-\bar{z}_1)\right)F(z_{1},z_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})\\
=&(-1)^{\lambda\circ\lambda^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{p\geq 0}(-1)^{p}z_{2}^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle-p}z_{1}^{p}\right)\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}\bar{z}_{2}^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle-q}\bar{z}_{1}^{q}\right)F(z_{1},z_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}),\end{split}

(237)

where we used the fact that in the principal branch of logarithm to write

\log(-z)=\log|z|+i(\pi+\text{Arg}(z)),\quad\log(-\bar{z})=\log|z|-i(\pi+\text{Arg}(z))

(238)

with

-\pi<\pi+\text{Arg}(z)<\pi.

(239)

{remark}

From the calculations above, it is easy to see that the following formal commutativity axiom holds for the vertex operators: there exists
K,\bar{K}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(x_{1}-x_{2})^{K}(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2})^{\bar{K}}\left[Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x_{1},\bar{x}_{1}),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})\right]=0.

(240)

Indeed we have

\begin{split}\left[Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x_{1},\bar{x}_{1}),\right.&\left.Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})\right]=\left(\left(x_{1}-x_{2}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right.\\
&\left.-(-1)^{\lambda\circ\lambda^{\prime}}\left(x_{2}-x_{1}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{x}_{1}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right)F(x_{1},x_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\\
=&\left(\left(x_{1}-x_{2}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right.\\
&\left.-\left(-x_{2}+x_{1}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(-\bar{x}_{2}+\bar{x}_{1}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right)F(x_{1},x_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\end{split}

(241)

Since
(\alpha,\beta),(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{0}
, we have
\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle,\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}
and we can choose
K,\bar{K}\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that

K+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{N},\quad\bar{K}+\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{N}.

(242)

We then get

\begin{split}(x_{1}-x_{2})^{K}(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2})^{\bar{K}}&\left[Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x_{1},\bar{x}_{1}),Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}},x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})\right]\\
=\left((x_{1}-x_{2})^{K+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\right.&\left.(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2})^{\bar{K}+\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right.\\
-&\left.\left(-x_{2}+x_{1}\right)^{K+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(-\bar{x}_{2}+\bar{x}_{1}\right)^{\bar{K}+\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right)F(x_{1},x_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\\
=\left((x_{1}-x_{2})^{K+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\right.&\left.(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2})^{\bar{K}+\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right.\\
-&\left.\left(x_{1}-x_{2}\right)^{K+\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right)^{\bar{K}+\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}\right)F(x_{1},x_{2})\bar{F}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\\
=0.\end{split}

(243)

We now prove the locality for general
vertex operators.

{thm}

The vertex operators
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})
, where
v
is the general vector of
V_{\Lambda}
, satisfy the locality property
9
. More precisely there exists, multi-valued, operator-valued functions
f(z_{1},z_{2})
and
g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})
analytic in
z_{1},z_{2}
and
\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}
respectively with possible singularities at
\{(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\,\lvert\,z_{1},z_{2}\neq 0,z_{1}\neq z_{2}\}
, such that
f(z_{1},z_{2})g(\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2})
is single-valued when
\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2}
are the complex conjugates of
z_{1},z_{2}
respectively and equals

\begin{split}Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(w,z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\ {\textrm{w}hen}\ \lvert z_{1}\rvert>\lvert z_{2}\rvert,\\
Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(w,z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\ {\textrm{w}hen}\ \lvert z_{2}\rvert>\lvert z_{1}\rvert.\end{split}

(244)

{proof}

We will prove the locality for the spanning set of vectors of the form (
152
). Explicitly, we will prove the locality for vertex operators of the form

\begin{split}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x,\bar{x})=\typecolon\prod_{r=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{\bar{k}}\left(\frac{1}{(m_{r}-1)!}\frac{d^{m_{r}-1}\alpha_{r}(x)}{dx^{m_{r}-1}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{m}_{s}-1)!}\frac{d^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}\beta_{s}(\bar{x})}{d\bar{x}^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}}\right)Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\typecolon\\
&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(w,x,\bar{x})=\typecolon\prod_{p=1}^{\ell}\prod_{q=1}^{\bar{\ell}}\left(\frac{1}{(n_{p}-1)!}\frac{d^{n_{p}-1}\alpha_{p}^{\prime}(x)}{dx^{n_{p}-1}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{n}_{q}-1)!}\frac{d^{\bar{n}_{q}-1}\beta_{q}^{\prime}(\bar{x})}{d\bar{x}^{\bar{n}_{q}-1}}\right)Y_{V_{\Lambda}}({\rm e}^{\lambda},x,\bar{x})\typecolon,\end{split}

(245)

see
Dolan:1989vr
for a similar calculation.
Following the exact same steps as in the proof of
(
huang
, Eq. (2.14))
with appropriate modifications, we can show that

\left[\alpha^{\prime}\left(x_{1}\right)^{+},{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{2}\right)^{-}dx_{2}}\right]=\left(\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{x_{1}-x_{2}}-\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{x_{1}}\right){\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{-}dx_{1}}.

(246)

Differentiating on both the sides of (

\left[\frac{1}{s!}\frac{d^{s}\alpha^{\prime}\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}}{dx_{1}^{s}},{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{2}\right)^{-}dx_{2}}\right]=(-1)^{s}\left(\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{s+1}}-\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{x_{1}^{s+1}}\right){\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{-}dx_{1}}.

(247)

Differentiating both sides of (

\left[\frac{1}{s!}\frac{d^{s}\alpha^{\prime}\left(x_{2}\right)^{-}}{dx_{2}^{s}},{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}\right]=\frac{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{s+1}}{\rm e}^{\int\alpha\left(x_{1}\right)^{+}dx_{1}}.

(248)

Analogous formula holds for
[\beta^{\prime}(\bar{x}_{1})^{\pm},\exp(\int\beta(x_2)^{\mp})]
. In addition, we need

\alpha(0){\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}+{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}\alpha(0),\quad\lambda^{\prime}=(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}).

This follows from the following calculation: for
u\in S(\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{-})
,

\displaystyle\alpha(0){\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\epsilon(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})}x^{\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}\rangle\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\epsilon(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})}x^{\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)+(-1)^{\epsilon(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})}x^{\langle\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime\prime}\rangle\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\,{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)+{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x^{\alpha^{\prime}}\alpha(0)\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right).

Analogous formulas for

\begin{split}\beta(0)&{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\bar{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\left(u\otimes{\rm e}^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\right)\\

\begin{split}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x_{1},\bar{x}_{1})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(w,x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})=\exp\left(\int\alpha(x_{1})^{-}\right)\exp\left(\int\beta(\bar{x}_{1})^{-}\right)\\
&\times\typecolon\prod_{r=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{\bar{k}}\left(\frac{1}{(m_{r}-1)!}\frac{d^{m_{r}-1}\alpha_{r}(x_{1})}{dx_{1}^{m_{r}-1}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{m}_{s}-1)!}\frac{d^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}\beta_{s}(\bar{x}_{1})}{d\bar{x}_{1}^{\bar{m}_{s}-1}}\right)\typecolon\\

\begin{split}&Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(w,x_{2},\bar{x}_{2})Y_{V_{\Lambda}}(v,x_{1},\bar{x}_{1})=\left(x_{2}-x_{1}\right)^{\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle}\left(\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{x}_{1}\right)^{\langle\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rangle}(-1)^{\lambda\circ\lambda^{\prime}}\\

&\times{\rm e}_{\lambda}{\rm e}_{\lambda^{\prime}}x_{1}^{\alpha}\bar{x}_{1}^{\beta}x_{2}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\bar{x}_{2}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,,\end{split}

(256)

where we used (
159
). Now, let us take
x_{1},x_{2},\bar{x}_{1}
and
\bar{x}_{2}
to be complex numbers
z_{1},z_{2},\bar{z}_{1}
and

respectively. Then we can rewrite (
